## 2026-09-30 03:43 — #13 real-utility research: token integrity monitor DESIGNED + BUILT (shipped as loop tooling, no site deploy)

- **The idea (genuinely new, additive):** the hub publishes LOCKED canonical claims on many surfaces (hero, swap box, /story, /roots ProofStrip, whitepaper, Dumb AI knowledge): DUMB 6% / DAMP 3% transfer tax, the feesDistribution splits, owner renounced. Every claim was verified on-chain once (2026-09-26/27) — but nothing re-verifies them, so if the chain truth ever diverged (or our own constants drifted in a future edit), the hub would silently publish false claims. A read-only loop-side integrity monitor closes that gap: it is the institutional version of the standing honesty rule ("verified on-chain, never invented").
- **Utility-bar evaluation:** real repeated pain = YES (the community's core wound is false claims about these tokens — the /story page exists to correct the official X account; newcomers' first question is "is what's on this site still true?"). Brings outsiders in = weakly (trust surface, not a draw). Burns supply = NO — it is maintenance-of-honesty, closer to #9 than #13, but it deepens holding confidence and protects every other utility's credibility. Vetoes: none touched (read-only, no custody, no new data source — public RPC already in the trust boundary, no spending, no signing, no new contract).
- **Evidence (live, read-only, this run):** DUMB fees()=600, feesDistribution()=(3333,3333,1667,1667), owner()=0x0; DAMP fees()=300, feesDistribution()=(3333,0,3333,3334), owner()=0x0 — ALL match the locked canonical values. Informational: DUMB supply 28B, dead 15.84B (56.6% burned); DAMP supply 1B, dead 364M (36.4% burned).
- **Shipped:** `~/workspace/goals/pulsechain-dumb-damp-build-loop/hidden_files/token-integrity-check.py` — ~100 lines, exit 0 when every asserted claim matches, exit 1 + DEVIATION line otherwise. Retries/backoff per the documented rpc.pulsechain.com flakiness (batches die; 1s between calls, 5 attempts, 5-25s backoff across both RPCs). Runs from the loop on #9 security passes — no site code touched, no deploy needed.
- **Considered and REJECTED:** (1) a user-facing "contract watch" freshness strip — REJECTED: the #16 ProofStrip already gives every visitor live figures with block number + age at 60s refetch; a second strip would duplicate it = churn. The durable value is the loop-side alert, not another UI widget. (2) asserting on dead-balance totals — REJECTED: balances legitimately grow with every burn; only the tax/owner claims are assertable. (3) on-chain monitoring via events/subscriptions — REJECTED: the loop itself is the scheduler; a push-based watcher adds infra for zero marginal value at 10-minute loop cadence.

## 2026-09-30 19:33 — #13 real-utility research: embeddable scan-verdict badge considered, REJECTED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW utility angle — embeddable token-safety badge:** a small image/link a token project could embed on its own page showing its hub scan verdict ("Scanned on the DUMB hub: no mint power, tax 6%") — the distribution theory being that other communities' pages become inbound funnels to /scan, and every deep scan behind it burns DUMB. **REJECTED:** (1) Safety — a badge is a frozen claim. The verdict would need live re-scan at view time — and that already exists: the shareable report-card links shipped in scanner phase 3 (2026-09-27) carry a hash/address, never a frozen result, so a shared link can never show a stale story (same principle as TxExplainer). The badge adds a hazard, not a capability. (2) Demand — 3 deep scans in 30 days is the entire measured base; there is no project asking to embed anything. (3) It also edges toward an endorsement surface (hub branding on third-party token pages), which needs Antonio's call, not a loop ship. Parked permanently unless verdicts become live-rendered AND real projects ask.

## 2026-09-30 20:53 — #8 bullish-demand research: refer-a-friend burn rebate considered and REJECTED

- **Angle considered:** a refer-a-friend mechanic — invite links where a newcomer's first voluntary burn credits the referrer on a public invite leaderboard, with a small DUMB "rebate" back to the referrer.
- **REJECTED:** (1) the rebate is a funded incentive — paying it needs either Antonio spending (hard boundary: spending = his approval) or a contract/treasury skimming burns, which breaks the "every burn is destroyed, nothing skimmed" honesty the hub sells; (2) it creates a sybil surface (self-referrals through fresh wallets cost only gas + the 6% tax, and the leaderboard would fill with one actor's alts — invented-enthusiasm by construction); (3) rebate-for-action reads as a promised return on participation, brushing the standing "never promise gains / no funded prize" veto; (4) the base is zero — 35 crawler-grade pv today, 3 deep scans/30d, zero share_tap events ever; there is nobody to refer yet. ## 2026-09-30 21:13 — #13 real-utility research: bulk scanner considered and REJECTED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW utility angle — bulk scanner (paste up to 10 token addresses, side-by-side safety table):** the pitch is throughput — a trader checking a watchlist or a community vetting several launches could paste a list and get one comparison table instead of ten single scans. **REJECTED** on four grounds. (1) **RPC amplification:** the quick scan is client-side today (`src/lib/token-scan.ts`, ~22 checks per token hitting the public PulseChain RPC with `eth_call` + `eth_getLogs`); the standing AGENTS.md note documents `rpc.pulsechain.com` dropping connections under batch/repeated calls. Ten-at-once multiplies that load by an order of magnitude, inviting the exact flake class the hub already mitigates on /api/market, and would degrade the single-scan path that actually exists. (2) **Honesty / ranking hazard:** a side-by-side table reads as a safety league table. The hub never ranks or blesses tokens — verdicts are per-token live snapshots that re-run at view time (shareable report cards carry an address, never a frozen verdict). A cached comparison freezes ten mutable verdicts into one screenshot that can go stale while the hub's name sits on it — the same failure the embeddable-badge rejection closed. (3) **Zero demand base:** 3 deep scans in 30 days and 1 quick_scan event today on 35 crawler-grade pageviews. There is no measured single-scan audience yet; building throughput before audience is reversed sequencing. (4) **No burn leg:** bulk quick scans are free by design; the deep-scan burn gate is per-token for a reason (verified 100k DUMB burn unlocks a 30-day pass) — a bulk surface either bypasses that gate or creates pay-per-list confusion, neither of which deepens holding. Parked until single-scan volume is 10× and real users ask for batch.

## 2026-09-30 22:03 — #13 real-utility research: token name/symbol lookup for /scan considered and REJECTED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW utility angle — let /scan accept a token NAME or SYMBOL instead of requiring a pasted contract address:** pitch is newcomer convenience — people know "DUMB" or "PLSX" but copying a 42-character address feels technical, and a search box feels friendlier. The hub's scanner already leans on DexScreener server-side (/api/market), so a DexScreener name-search would not be a wholly new data source. **REJECTED on safety grounds — the address-paste gate is a deliberate safety design, not a UX oversight.** (1) **Impersonation is the whole scam model:** name/symbol lookup is exactly how scammers operate — a malicious token copies a trusted name ("DUMB", "DAMP", "PLSX"), and a search box returns whichever copy ranks first; the paste-an-address flow forces the user to operate on the exact contract they intend, and the hub already pins the real DUMB/DAMP addresses with ContractGuardrail. Trading that precision for convenience hands scammers the attack surface the hub exists to close. (2) **Ambiguity resolution is a support surface:** a name returning three lookalikes needs a disambiguation UI, trust-ranked ordering, and a support story when someone scans the wrong one — none of which exist, and each of which is a new way for a user to get rekt while trusting the hub's framing. (3) **Zero demand to underwrite it:** 3 deep scans/30d, 1 quick_scan event today on 35 crawler-grade pageviews — the paste box has no measured friction, because there are no measured users. Rebuild the front door only after real visitors report the lock is the problem. Stays rejected unless real user feedback asks for it, and even then with an explicit disambiguation safety design.

## 2026-09-30 22:53 — #13 real-utility research: reflections-earnings tracker considered and REJECTED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW utility angle — a read-only "reflections you've earned" tracker:** pitch is retention — DUMB/DAMP pay ~1% reflections to holders on every taxed transfer, so a wallet page showing "your holdings grew by X since you connected" gives holders a reason to return and deepens holding without forcing the token into payments. **REJECTED.** (1) **No honest baseline exists:** reflections arrive as silent balance growth inside the wallet; measuring "earned" requires a first-seen balance snapshot per wallet, stored server-side, then diffed against later balances — but balance changes also come from buys, sells, and transfers, so the diff cannot attribute growth to reflections from balance reads alone. Untangling it means full transfer-history reconstruction per wallet (heavy RPC, and the public RPC is documented batch-flaky). Any simpler number would be an estimate presented as earnings — invented precision, which the honesty rules forbid. (2) **Framing risk:** a running "you earned X" counter reads as yield/returns on the hub's own pages — the standing veto on anything promising returns exists precisely for this; even an accurate figure would market the token as an income product. (3) **New stored state for zero measured demand:** per-wallet snapshots are a new server-side data surface (KV writes keyed to wallets) built for a holder base that today measures 2 wallet_connect events on 35 crawler-grade pageviews. The existing /wallet holdings read already shows the live balance honestly. Parked permanently unless a clean on-chain attribution method exists AND Antonio wants holder-yield framing on the hub — his call, not a loop ship.

## 2026-10-01 00:53 — #13 real-utility research: printable/PDF scan-report export considered and REJECTED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW utility angle — a downloadable/printable PDF export of a token safety scan:** pitch is shareability beyond the hub — a user could carry a one-page plain-English safety report into a Telegram group, a Discord, or an offline conversation where a link won't be clicked. **REJECTED.** (1) **A PDF freezes a verdict that can rot:** tax rates, owner powers, and liquidity locks are mutable on non-renounced contracts; the hub's already-shipped shareable report-card links deliberately re-scan at view time (#12 phase 3), while a PDF would keep asserting "safe" months after the contract changed — the exact stale-safety-claim failure the safest-hub north star exists to prevent (same ground as the 19:33 embed-badge rejection). (3) **New rendering surface for no burn:** a server-side PDF pipeline is real code and attack surface (user-supplied token metadata rendered into a document) that neither burns DUMB nor brings outsiders in on its own. Revisit only if report-card link sharing shows real use AND viewers ask for a portable copy; even then, any export must carry the scan timestamp and "re-check — this can change" framing.

## 2026-10-01 03:13 — #13 real-utility research: token-approval (allowance) auditor considered and REJECTED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW utility angle — an approvals auditor on /wallet ("who can spend your tokens?"):** stale or unlimited ERC20 allowances are the classic quiet-drain vector, so a read-only auditor listing every spender approved over the connected wallet's DUMB/DAMP (flag the unlimited ones, offer a revoke) pitches as pure north-star safety — a newcomer checks before they get rekt. **REJECTED on four grounds.** (1) **Spender discovery is impractical read-only:** `allowance(owner, spender)` is a point read that requires already knowing the spender; enumerating "everyone who can spend" means scanning `Approval` event logs across the token's entire life (tens of millions of blocks) against the documented batch-flaky public RPC — the hub's own BurnTicker stays inside 20k-block windows precisely because wide log scans are unreliable here. Any shortcut (known spenders only) silently under-reports. (2) **Partial truth is worse than none:** a list that misses one rogue spender reads as an all-clear — false assurance from the hub whose entire positioning is "don't get rekt." (3) **The hub's own surface already engineers this risk out:** SwapBox approves the EXACT parsed input amount per swap (verified in repeated #9 passes), so hub-originated allowances land at zero after each trade; the residual risk lives in approvals granted on other dApps — exactly the set the hub cannot enumerate. (4) **The actionable half is out of bounds and there is no demand:** revoke = a wallet-signed `approve(spender, 0)` transaction, a new signing surface beyond what exists (hard boundary — Antonio's approval), and a display-only list whose only action is "go elsewhere" is half a tool; meanwhile demand is 0 pv today, 2 wallet_connect events on 2026-09-30's 35 crawler-grade pageviews, empty feedback inbox. Revisit only if real wallet traffic appears AND Antonio approves a revoke signing surface; even then, scope to named spenders (the PulseX router) with copy that says exactly that, never "all clear."

## 2026-10-01 03:53 — #13 real-utility research: interactive "spot the scam" phishing simulator considered and PARKED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW utility angle — a clearly-labeled interactive training page that walks a newcomer through a FAKE airdrop-claim scam site:** a simulated "claim your free tokens" dApp (fake connect wallet, fake unlimited-approval prompt, fake drained wallet) with educational annotations at each step naming the red flag, never touching a real wallet, never signing anything, never sending a transaction. Read-only, zero new contracts, zero new data sources, zero signing surface — the simulation is entirely fake client state.
- **PARKED, not rejected outright, on three grounds.** (1) **Zero measured demand for education content:** the existing education page (/story) drew 3 crawler-grade views across all of 2026-09-30, the feedback inbox is empty, and 2026-10-01 sits at 0 pageviews at run time. Building an elaborate training room for arrivals that don't exist yet is the same funnel-first error the 00:03 checklist park flagged — the measured bottleneck is still discovery, not education supply. (2) **Framing risk is real:** a convincing fake phishing page is a liability the moment a screenshot travels without its SIMULATION labels; the safest-hub's north star forbids shipping a page that could ever be mistaken for the thing it warns about without Antonio's explicit content call in his own voice. (3) **Utility bar is not cleanly met:** it neither burns DUMB per use nor deepens holding; "brings outsiders in" is its only plausible leg, and that's speculative until measured arrivals exist. Revisit only if real newcomers start asking scam-avoidance questions in feedback, or Antonio wants an education pillar — his voice, his call, never a loop ship.

(3) **Utility bar not met:** a watchlist link itself burns nothing and deepens no holding; its only leg is "brings outsiders in," which is speculative until arrivals and share behavior are measured. Not a veto — revisit if watchlist toggles become regular real events in KV or users ask to share lists in feedback; the safe shape would be addresses-only, no verdicts in the URL, decoded client-side with live re-scan.

## 2026-10-01 04:53 — #8 bullish-demand research: printable QR share aids considered and PARKED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW demand angle — printable QR codes for the /scan and /burn links on the hub:** pitch is an offline-to-online bridge — a community member at a meetup, conference table, or in a printed flyer context points a phone camera at a QR and lands directly on the scanner or the burn page, no typing, no searching. In principle it brings outsiders in through a channel the hub's digital share surfaces (share text, native share, report-card links) don't reach. **PARKED, not shipped, on three grounds.** (1) **Zero measured offline demand:** the feedback inbox holds only the loop's own disclosed probe entry, today sits at 0 pageviews, and `share_tap` has never fired — there is no evidence anyone is encountering the hub offline and failing to reach it; building print plumbing for an unmeasured channel repeats the sequencing error flagged for the embeddable widget (01:03) and the bookmarklet (02:03). (2) **The implementation is not free on the safest hub:** a correct QR encoder is either a new runtime dependency (new external dependency = hard boundary, Antonio's call) or a hand-rolled encoder whose correctness the loop's curl-level live checks can't verify — a malformed code that fails to scan, or worse scans somewhere unexpected, is a trust defect. (3) **The digital path already covers the measured surface:** share text + NativeShareButton + Post to X / Share to Telegram + symbol-only report-card links already carry the links where the community actually lives (X/Telegram); a QR adds a physical-world hop with no measured audience on the other side. Not a veto — revisit if Antonio starts doing in-person events or feedback asks for printable material; the safe shape would be client-side generation from the canonical URL only, no verdicts or numbers encoded beyond the link itself.

## 2026-10-01 05:43 — #8 bullish-demand research: hub Chrome extension ("Scan with Dumb Money Hub") considered and PARKED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW demand angle — a hub-branded Chrome/Chromium extension that meets users where they research tokens:** a "Scan with Dumb Money Hub" action (right-click a selected address, or a button injected next to tokens on explorer/chart pages) that opens the existing `/scan?token=…` report link, which already re-runs the quick scan live on-chain at view time (`src/routes/scan.tsx:19`). The channel is real — competitor research earlier tonight (03:23/04:53 entries) found OpenPulseChain already ships a Chrome extension alongside its safety API, so browser-resident scanning is a proven PulseChain distribution surface the hub has never evaluated for itself. The extension would add NO new scanning capability: it is a launcher into the deep link that already exists, with zero new data sources, zero custody, zero signing.
- **PARKED, not shipped, on four grounds.** (1) **Trust inversion at the safest hub:** an extension needs host/read permissions over pages the user browses — including wallet-adjacent ones — and lookalike or over-privileged extensions are a classic drain vector. The hub teaches "never install things that can read your wallet pages"; asking newcomers to install hub software that can read the pages they research on cuts against the north star even if the code is minimal and open. (2) **Hard boundaries:** a Web Store listing is external representation under the project name (listing copy, screenshots, support surface) and any developer-account/store step that involves a fee is spending — both are Antonio's explicit calls, never loop ships; the loop does not post or publish as the project. (3) **Zero measured demand to distribute to:** today is 0 pv at run time, `/api/scan-stats` is flat at {deep_scans:3, pass_burns:1, dumb_destroyed_min:100000}, `share_tap` has never fired, and the feedback inbox holds only the loop's own disclosed probe — a store listing is distribution plumbing for users who have not arrived, the same sequencing error as the embeddable widget (01:03) and bookmarklet (02:03) parks. (4) **Standing maintenance surface:** store review, permission-change re-review, DOM fragility against third-party chart/explorer pages, and an update supply chain to defend — an ongoing burden that burns no DUMB and deepens no holding on its own. Distinct from the 02:03 bookmarklet park: a bookmarklet is user-carried page code with no store identity, while an extension is installable software under the project's name with browser-granted page access — worse on trust, better on discoverability, still un-earned today. Not a veto — revisit only if real scan volume exists (sustained human quick/deep scans in KV, share_tap firing) AND Antonio explicitly wants a browser presence and accepts the store listing in his name; the safe shape would be a minimal manifest whose only action opens the canonical `/scan?token=…` link — no page scraping, no inline verdicts, no wallet-page access.

## 2026-10-01 10:13 — #13 real-utility research: plain-English transaction explainer considered and PARKED (research-only, no ship)

- **NEW angle:** a "What just happened in this tx?" explainer — paste any PulseChain tx hash and get a plain-English decode of what actually moved: each Transfer leg, the tax split, what landed at the dead address, and whether a burn/swap/send did what the user expected. The hub already decodes exactly these legs internally (burn classification in `src/lib/burns.ts`, receipt verification behind /api/brag-image and the deep-scan pass), so the decoding core exists; this would generalize it from hub burns to arbitrary transactions.
- **PARKED, not shipped:** (1) zero measured demand for the adjacent surface — the scanner that would feed it has 3 deep scans and 1 pass burn in 30 days, `share_tap` has never fired, and today's live proxies are flat (scan-stats unchanged, single unredeemed pledge, all routes 200 — verified not an outage); nobody has ever pasted a tx hash into the feedback inbox or asked "what did my tx do" through any channel the hub can see. (2) The real version is a generality rabbit hole — honest decoding of arbitrary contracts means labeling unknown contracts as unknown, and a half-right explainer on the "safest hub" is worse than none; the safe subset (DUMB/DAMP/PulseX-router txs) is already answered where it matters: /burn verifies arrivals, the scanner simulates round-trips, and scan.pulsechain.com receipts are one click from every hub surface that shows a tx. (3) The burn linkage is the weakest of any utility considered — an explainer is read-once post-hoc; a burn-gated "deep decode" tier would gate the exact moment a worried newcomer needs reassurance, which trades the north star (feel safe, don't get rekt) for a toll. Free-only, it burns nothing and deepens no holding. Distinct from the 04:33 watchlist park (monitoring future state) and the 06:03 transfer-net preview (pre-send simulation): this is post-hoc receipt translation, parked on its own merits.
- **Revisit only if:** a real "what did my transaction do" ask lands (feedback, Telegram, or a support thread Antonio sees), deep-scan/pass volume climbs off the floor of 3, or the hub ships another flow whose receipts users must verify. Safe shape then: curated-contracts-only explainer (DUMB/DAMP + PulseX V2 router + dead-address legs), every unknown leg labeled unknown in plain English, free — never burn-gated at the reassurance moment.

## 2026-10-01 10:23 — #8 bullish-demand research: burn-milestone alerts considered and PARKED (research-only, no ship)

- **Idea:** opt-in alerts when total destroyed crosses the next milestone (16B DUMB, next DAMP mark) — browser push notification and/or a personal watch link that pings the holder, so the milestone moment brings people back to /burn without Antonio having to catch it live.

An alert system for nobody fails code minimalism. (2) The next milestone is ~161,237,998 DUMB away (dead 15,838,762,002 vs 16B); at the recent trickle (~17,711 in the busiest recent hour, most hours zero) the first alert would sit silent for months — a subscribed-but-never-firing feature reads as broken on a hub whose promise is that everything shown is real and live. (3) Every delivery shape crosses a hard boundary: browser push requires a service worker (the same stale-JS / stale-contract-address risk that parked the PWA on 2026-10-01 07:33 — on a wallet-adjacent hub, serving a stale address is the worst failure mode); a Telegram bot or email list is a new external data source/dependency plus a subscriber store (PII) the hub's privacy posture ("no cookies, no tracking, no wallet data") deliberately avoids; announcing the signup is outbound communication as Antonio. (4) It burns nothing and deepens no holding — it only notifies about other people's burns, the weakest demand linkage of any angle considered this week.
- **Distinct from prior parks:** not the 2026-10-01 09:43 weekly "State of the Burn" snapshot (a dated public page for posting), not the 2026-10-01 08:23 Burn Hour (convening a crowd), not the 2026-10-01 09:23 OBS overlay (streamer chrome). This one is per-user alerting infrastructure.
- **Revisit only if:** real burn velocity or repeat-visitor traffic appears in KV (events firing, share_tap used), a milestone comes within plausible reach (weeks, not months), or Antonio wants to run a milestone moment himself — safe shape then: no service worker, no stored emails; a self-contained watch page (bookmarkable, reads the same dead-balance the hub already reads) that Antonio links from his own post.

## 2026-10-01 10:43 — #8 bullish-demand research: verified-burn receipt permalink considered and PARKED (research-only, no ship)

- **Idea:** a stable, citable hub permalink per burn transaction (e.g. /burn/tx/<hash>) that renders, in plain English, the same verification /api/brag-image performs — amount that arrived at the dead address, voluntary-vs-protocol classification, block, and an explorer link — so a burner has one durable hub URL to cite in any chat instead of a raw explorer link. The distribution logic is genuine: burners already get a brag image for X/Telegram unfurls, but there is no hub-native citable receipt; the ticker rows link straight out to scan.pulsechain.com, so the hub's one proof-shaped moment currently hands its audience to the explorer.
- **Verdict: PARKED, not a veto.** (1) **Zero measured demand for the event itself** — /api/scan-stats flat ({deep_scans:3, pass_burns:1, dumb_destroyed_min:100000}), the pledge board still holds the single real pledge (too_dumb, 1000 DUMB, unredeemed), and the brag image — the existing verified-burn share surface — has effectively never been used; a receipt page for an event that does not happen is dead UI on the safest hub. (2) **The facts are already one click away** — BurnTicker rows link each burn's receipt on the explorer, and /api/brag-image verifies arrivals; a permalink duplicates the explorer receipt under hub chrome, real machinery (per-request receipt + Transfer-log decode + classification + an honest not-found state for txs outside the ticker's 20,000-block window) that fails code minimalism for zero measured users. (3) **Framing travels detached** — a hub-branded "verified" URL gets pasted without /burn's locked honesty context ("burns shrink supply — never promise price") and reads as the hub vouching wherever it lands; the one thing in its favor is that a burn is a historical fact and cannot rot like a scan verdict (the same property that makes brag images honest), so the endorsement risk is thinner than the rejected verdict-share family — the park rests on zero demand + duplication, not on rot. (4) **It burns nothing and deepens no holding** — it is a nicer link for other people's burns, the weakest demand linkage of any angle considered this week.
- **Distinct from prior parks:** not /api/brag-image (a dynamic OG image for unfurls; this is a citable HTML page, no image), not the 09:43 dated weekly "State of the Burn" snapshot (aggregate dated page vs a single-tx permalink), not the 01:23 rejected copy-text block (detached text vs a page that re-verifies at view time), not the 10:23 milestone alerts (per-user notification vs a passive URL).
- **Revisit only if:** brag shares or share_tap ever fire, real voluntary-burn volume appears in the ticker, or a burner asks for a citable receipt. Safe shape then: read-only, re-verify at view time from the receipt + Transfer logs (the same decode as src/lib/burns.ts), locked "burns shrink supply — never promise price" framing on the page, honest not-found state, no invented data.
- **Revisit scan:** no previously parked/rejected item has ripened — scan-stats and pledges unchanged (above); milestone alerts (10:23), the weekly snapshot (09:43), the OBS overlay (09:23), the walkthrough video (09:03), the avatar frame (07:53), the PWA (07:33), and campaign tags (06:59) all remain blocked on the same zero-measured-demand signal; aggregator profiles and gift-a-pass remain Antonio's call (outward communication / promo, hard boundaries untouched).
